                                 Description/Sketch of Object

                                                                    . .-<>· . ..


                                                                                             ----

           I observed two stars in N. W. sky just after sunset that were obviously
           different to other stars appearing at this time. These lights didn' t
           twinkle and were moving slowly            the horizon .
               have a mounted •Universe" telescope fitted with lens strength 20X.
           Upon inspection I could see these two lights were satellites or U. F. O' s.
             I kept observing from time to t i me until they disappeared below the
           horizon at about 8 p .m.     live on Slade Point itself, a vantage point
           100' above sea level overlooking the ocean with a 3600 view .
            The object on the left was the smaller of the two and appeared to have
                     or rays of some kind extending fromit. The object on the right
           was much larger and twards the end of the observation (the l ast t
           was clearly seen to be painted red. There was also a smaller light
           below and to the right of the larger           not visible to1he naked eye.
           This was reported to Townsville R. A.A. F. on Tuesday 30th May, 1978 to
           Flt. Lt . B. Read We explained that what I saw was •American Sky Lab."
           and "Pegasus• •

A member organisation of the Australian Co-Ordinatiun Section (ACOS) , Centre
for UFO Studios, USA.
         Report for 1g77,    -January 1978.
1977 :   May 1977 saw the official formation of UFO Research (FNQ), and since
't"hat month we have had some ctlanges in the organisations' committee.
 Our original Co-Ordinator, Mr. Nax Dickson moved back to Sydney, and our
 Liaison Officer and foun der Mrs. Di Van Wijk, left for a short period in the
 Solomons but has now returned to Cairns.
 The existing committee is now --- Co-Ordinator --- Nr. Daryl Knowles: ph. 671242
                                     Liaison Officer - Mrs. P. Sandercock: ph.
                         Assistant Liaison Officer - Ms. Holly Goriss: ph. 671297
                                     Treasurer ----- Mr. Russ Boundy: ph. 531919
Membership : At the start of January 197B, UFOR(FNQ) had a membership of 19.
We hope with continued support of our existing and future members to issue a
newsletter regularly in the near future, and we remind our · mambers that many
people are interested in 'UFOs ' and if you know someone who is, 'sign em. up'.
In line with other member organisations in the Australian Co-Ordination
Section (ACOS), we have a c ontinuing co-operation from Government Departments
and Local Media. The RAAF , Dept. of Transport, Meteorolo gy Dept., Weap ons
          and Loca l Police all give assistance on investigations        is
essential in sorting out the IFOs from the UFOs. And the local         have
proved more than helpful on several occasions relating to sightings.
U.F.O.R.(F.N.Q.) is now represented in Cairns, Babinda, and Cardwell, and it
is hoped more members will join from other centres .
The year 1977 and January 1978 have proved that UFOs are present in North
Queensland and probably mora numerous than we realise. It has bean the
experience of the InvastigatorA of UFOR(FNQ) , that many more sightings

are very interes ting but past proper investigation.

reports, four are still being investigated. These came from Innisfail, and
Hollowa ys Beach , from which there were three sightings          within a month.

1.         Ui th regard to Reference A, llX Griesber g of ACOS had a oeeting \·Ti th
ltt H. n . Rayner , Director of Public Relations , and DAPI- ILO on 8 I1arch 1976.
l'!X Griesberg appeared to be a fairly common sense sort of character Hho did
not believe in little green men in flying saucers , and his organization seemed
to be making a genuine effort to investigate 1 UF0s 1 \·lith the aim of providing
a rational explanation.

2.       Having spent some time impressing on llr Griesberg that      e RAAF did
not confiscate photographs or classify investigations Top Secret , it \Tould
have been pointless to then turn around and refuse to co- operate at all with
ACOS .   Therefore , as part of this public relations exercise , it was agreed
that , vTOrk load and time permitting, if AC0:3 did for\·lard any reports to the
neareot RAA.F Base then they v1ould be informed of the results of the RAAF 1 s
investigation.     It was emphasised that the RAAF would not enter into any
discussion with regard to the results of an investigation.

3.      \ihilst this policy may create a littl e nore \oJOrk at both Air Force
Office and Formations , it is felt that in the long run it co .ld make life
a lot eaoi er.
4.      In your reply to 11iss Goriss ,.,e suggest that you enclose the report
forms she has requested , and reiterate the relevant parts of             2 of this
letter concerning \·TOrlc load and non- discussion of findings .

     Dear Sir,

          This letter is to inform you of the formation of UFO RESEARCH - FAR
     NORTH QUEENSLAND (UFOR-FNQ) in Cairns.
          We are a civilian organisation investigating and researching unusual
     aerial sightings (UFOs) and are a member organisation of the AUSTRALIAN
     CD-ORDINATION SECTION (ACOS) of the CENTRE FOR UFO STUDIES (USA).
     Attached please find a circular informing you of the function of ACOS
     and its member organisations.

          We mainly function as a body to which members of the public can report
     sightings of UFOa to, and then set out to investigate them and find a natural
     explanation if one is possible. As you are well aware, approximately
     85 - 90% of all sightings can be identified as being such common items aa
     planes, weather balloons, planets, meteors, birds etc .

          The reason for this letter is to ask for      co-operation in
     investigating such reports, by us supplying you with details of any reports
     we receive and you advising us of any outcome of your investigations , as we
     will advise you should we find an explanation to any report forwarded to you •
                                                                                      ./)
          One of the Co-Ordinators of ACOS, Harry Griesberg, had a meeting with
     Mr. H. R. Rayner, Director of Public Relations RAAF at the Russell Offices
     in Canberra early last year, and it was agreed that the RAAF would co-operate
     with UFO organisations affiliated with ACOS in this way.
                                                                                      \.
          Would you therefore, kindly forward a supply of RAAF report forms
     which witnesses to Unusual Aerial sightings could fill out and which we
     would then forward on to you for your investigations.

                                             Looking forward to hearing from you,
                                             and hoping for a good working relationship,
                                             Yours sincerely,


                                             (Miss H. I . Goriss,)
                                             Assistant Liaison Officer,
                                             UFO Research Far North Queensland,

TO\INSVILLE, OLD.   4810.

     Sir,

         Your report of en unusual aerial sighting on 23rd           1974
has boen received and the investigating team           that two separate
light sources were sighted.

         Tl.p ini tiel sighting was probably t he e tar Rigel Ken taurus which
was visible with a mapnitude of 0 at an elevation of about 60° on a
bearing of about 165°.     TI1e second sighting was probably the planet Venue
which was visible with a mnenitude of -4 at an               of about 200 on
a bearing of about 95° .    Two factors are c onsidered to have caused the
         movemPnt of the light sources.                whilst travelling in a
car, the relative position of the lights, because of their distance from you,
would not chaneo when compared with much closer obje9ts, thus creating the
illusion of movement .    Secondly a phenomenon known as atmospheric scintillation,
caused by ohangoe in atmospheric density, can             apparent rapid movemf'nt
of stars and planets, and oan also cause increased               of such light
sources.                                              I
         The dioappearance of the two lights was probably due to either cloud
or natural objeots obscuring them from view.

         Wp regret the delay in replying and trust that this explanation is
satisfactory.


                                                Yours faithfUlly,


                                                (G. J . 0008RS)
